# Status

Implementation status of features specced in the other docs. One row per feature, identified by a stable kebab-case slug. Slugs are positional-free — they name the feature, not its location in the spec — so reorganizing sections of `editor.md`, `index.md`, etc. doesn't break references. Code or commit messages can cite a slug (e.g. "implements `pre-write-drift-check`") and the link survives doc reshuffles.

Status values:

- **done** — implemented and exercised
- **partial** — present but incomplete; the gap is named in the notes column
- **planned** — specced, not started

When a feature is reorganized, renamed, or split, update its row here first — this file is the registry. Audited 2026-05-06.


## How to use this file

- **When code implements (or starts implementing) a slug, tag it in a comment.** A short marker near the relevant entry point is enough: `// status: pre-write-drift-check` in Rust, `// status: drag-and-drop-move` in Rust. The goal is grep-ability — `rg "status: drag-and-drop-move"` should land you on the implementation.
- **Don't go overboard.** One tag per feature, near the most natural anchor (the public function, the event handler, the top of the relevant module). Don't sprinkle the slug across every helper. If a feature spans several files, tag the obvious entry point and let `status.md` be the index.
- **When you write a new spec, add its features here.** New rows go in the section for the spec doc that owns them, with a slug, status (`planned` to start), and a one-line note. If a feature crosses doc boundaries (e.g. a core command shared by UI and CLI), put it under the spec that defines its semantics and reference the slug from the others.
- **When a feature is renamed, split, or merged, edit `status.md` first**, then update tags in code and references in other docs. Treat the slug like a function name: stable until you deliberately rename it.

### Open meta-tasks

- [x] Backfilled spec docs with inline `[slug]` markers next to each feature definition — 2026-05-06.


### Bugs / known issues

Moved to [`bug_tracking.md`](bug_tracking.md). Same conventions (kebab-case slug, one-line note, optional file:line); this file stays focused on the feature registry.

| `editor-preview-tab-promotion` | done | `app/src/panels/buffer/mod.rs` (first-user-edit promotion), `app/src/workbench_host.rs` (tab-targeted promotion; double-click on the tab promotes; right-click menu prepends "Keep open" when the tab is preview) | promotion gate distinguishes user typing/paste/delete from programmatic doc swaps (file open, mutation apply); without that gate the tab would promote on the very first edit from opening the file. Drag-to-reorder isn't a promotion path today because tabs don't reorder yet — the spec lists drag, the implementation slots it in when reorder lands. | `navigation-swipe-visual-feedback` | done | `app/src/widgets/swipe_indicator.rs` (`show`), `app/src/main.rs` (call after panels), `app/src/state.rs` (`swipe_last_commit_dir`), `app/src/keybinds.rs` (decay + hold-at-threshold during cooldown) | rounded pill anchored on the swipe-toward edge (left for back, right for forward), with a chevron glyph and a progress-fill bar that grows with `\|swipe_accum_x\| / 120`. Greyed pill + chevron when there's no history in that direction (`nav_can_back` / `nav_can_forward`). On commit the accumulator is held at threshold through the 350ms cooldown so the fill flashes the accent colour; abandoned swipes decay exponentially toward 0 after 120ms of no input. Painted on `Order::Foreground` so it sits over all panels |
| `navigation-trackpad-swipe` | done | `app/src/keybinds.rs` (`handle_swipe_nav`), `app/src/state.rs` (`swipe_accum_x`, `swipe_cooldown_until`) | per-frame accumulator over `ctx.input(\|i\| i.smooth_scroll_delta.x)`; fires `editor_pane::nav_go(±1)` when `\|acc\| ≥ 120px` and locks for 350ms so one gesture isn't double-counted. Skipped entirely when any widget has focus (`ctx.memory(\|m\| m.focused().is_some())`) so a focused TextEdit / editor isn't hijacked. Horizontal-dominant gate (`\|dx\| > 1.5·\|dy\|`) filters diagonal scrolls; a vertical-dominant frame zeroes the accumulator so a later intentional swipe starts fresh. Positive `dx` (two-finger swipe right) → back; negative → forward. Browser-style mapping. Visual swipe overlay deferred per spec |
| `navigation-keybind` | done | `app/src/keybinds.rs` (`navigation.back` = `Mod-[`, `navigation.forward` = `Mod-]`, plus `Mod-[` / `Mod-]` outside the editor and Alt-Left / Alt-Right on every platform) | editor-focus case + the cross-pane handler cover tree / sidebar / status-bar focus and Linux/Windows browser-conventional Alt-Left/Right |
| `navigation-mouse-buttons` | planned | — | window-level `mousedown` / `auxclick` listener fires `navigation.back` on `event.button === 3` and `navigation.forward` on `event.button === 4` (the standard back/forward thumb buttons on side-button mice). Calls into the same action handlers as `navigation-keybind` and `navigation-trackpad-swipe` so all three trigger surfaces converge. Default-on; rebinding deferred until the keybind registry grows mouse-button support |

## Settings (settings.md)

v1 surface landed: TOML loader + auto-create + targeted write-back, editor / indexing / vault sections wired into the existing UI. The settings pane shell is also live: a gear button in the vault bar opens a sectioned page that flips eligible keys via `set_setting`, displays read-only keys with an "Open in file manager" affordance, and supports per-section scope toggling, per-row reset, manual refresh, and a `Cmd/Ctrl-,` keybind. The keymap section stays planned.

Requires an open op log. `WriteOutcome` carries optional `status` and `staging_id`; when absent, the default behavior is "written" (existing direct-write path) |
| `staging-accept-navigates-to-preview` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` (chat card accept → open note link), `app/src/panels/buffer/mod.rs` (staging-preview accept → open target in preview), `app/src/sidebar/files.rs` (tree-context accept → open). Activity-detail surface deliberately does *not* navigate — accept reloads the buffer in place if the target is the active file. Batch accept stays on the current surface |


## Clustering (clustering.md)

All planned. The build engine consumed by `suggestions.md`. Lands post-v1.

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `cluster-build-recipe` | done | `core/src/cluster.rs::build_cluster_tree` drives the top-down divisive Split recipe end-to-end. Step 1: a virtual-root partition over every note's embedding using `LeidenParams.top_level_resolution` (default 0.3) for the coarse top-level cut, then per-top-level-community recursive sub-split via `recursive_split_branch` using the regular `LeidenParams.resolution` (default 1.0) at sub-levels. Stop conditions per-branch: member count `<=` `leaf_min_size` OR cohesion radius `<` `leaf_cohesion_threshold` OR 16-level depth cap OR sub-partition produced <2 communities. `min_clusters_to_recurse` is deserialized for back-compat (`#[serde(default, skip_serializing)]`) and ignored at runtime. Step 2 (Summarize) is invoked per-cluster inline by the build's `summarizer` arg when callers pass `SummarizeMode::Llm`; the review tab's structural pass forces `SummarizeMode::None` so step 2 is deferred to Confirm-and-name. Step 3 (Rollup) is an explicit cluster-editor verb (`cluster-op-rollup`), not invoked by the build. Output `BuiltClusterTree` packs each cluster into `levels[max_child_level + 1]` (leaves at level 0, parents above) and adds a synthetic `vault-root` cluster at the top when >1 top-level communities are produced — so `result_to_node_inserts`'s "top.len() == 1 → that's root" path always lands a single root. Top-level outliers get force-routed when `include_outliers = false` (threshold `-1.0`) or hybrid-recovered at ≥0.6 cosine for `algorithm = Hybrid`; sub-split outliers fold back into the first child cluster as plain members. End-to-end consumer: the review tab's Run-clustering / Confirm-and-name / Confirm-no-naming buttons via `cluster_run_structural` → `build_tree_structural` → `build_tree` → `build_cluster_tree`. **Note:** the recipe's algorithmic core is duplicated between `core::cluster::recursive_split_branch` (this op, operates on `Vec<NoteInput>`, returns `BuiltClusterTree`) and `core/src/trees/ops/split.rs::Trees::split_cluster_recursive` (the user-driven verb, operates on persisted leaf rows + DB writes interleaved per recursion level for undo). Sharing more than the `partition_indices` primitive isn't worth the abstraction cost given the two sides' different output shapes; revisited during the `trees.rs` module-directory split and the verdict held — rationale captured in `core/src/trees/ops/split.rs`'s top-of-file comment |
| `cluster-note-embeddings` | done | `core/src/store/chunks.rs::upsert_note` computes the byte-length-weighted mean-pool from the chunks being written and persists it on `notes.note_embedding` in the same transaction (empty-chunk notes stay NULL). `Store::note_embedding_for_path` is the read path used by the cluster-review graph view (`app/src/panels/cluster_review/mod.rs`) and the cluster-tree sidebar leaf-preview. `Store::compute_and_store_note_embedding` and `Store::clear_note_embedding` remain on the surface for tests / one-off recomputes but the production write path is the inline-pool branch. `ensure_chunk_vecs_dim` still clears `note_embedding` when the embedder dim changes so a model swap can't leave stale-dim blobs around |
| `cluster-note-embeddings-direct-long-context` | planned | — | when a long-context embedder is selected (`bge-m3` / `embedding-gemma-300m` per `embedder-model-selectable`), embed the full note text directly instead of mean-pooling chunk vectors; mean-pool stays as the fallback for notes exceeding the model's context |
| `cluster-hdbscan` | done | `core/src/cluster.rs::partition` is the HDBSCAN entry; the build pipeline (`cluster-build-recursive`) is the first consumer. Determinism comes from petal-clustering's seeded fit + the stable L2-normalize → ndarray pipeline; outliers carry `OUTLIER_LABEL` and end up in `BuiltClusterTree.outliers` when `include_outliers = true` |
| `cluster-hdbscan-crate-petal` | partial | `core/src/cluster.rs::partition` — wraps `petal_clustering::HDbscan` over an L2-normalized `ndarray::Array2<f64>` so the crate's default Euclidean metric is monotonic with cosine (matches spec's "cosine distance via pre-normalized embeddings"); returns `Vec<ClusterAssignment>` with `cluster_label = OUTLIER_LABEL (-1)` for noise points. `l2_normalize` + `cosine_similarity` exposed for downstream use. Cargo workspace gains `petal-clustering 0.13` + `ndarray 0.17` (pinned to the version petal-clustering depends on). **Gap:** wired through `core::cluster::partition` but no consumer yet — `core::cluster::build_tree` (per `cluster-build-recursive`) is the first caller |
| `cluster-algorithm-selectable` | partial | `core/src/cluster.rs::ClusterAlgorithm` enum (`Hdbscan` / `Gmm` / `Hybrid` / `Leiden`) selectable on `ClusterParams.algorithm`; persisted on the tree row via `cluster_trees.method` JSON. `Hdbscan` runs end-to-end; `Hybrid` runs HDBSCAN + the outlier-recovery reassignment pass below; `Leiden` runs end-to-end via `partition_leiden` over a kNN cosine-similarity graph (`cluster-leiden`). **Gap:** `Gmm` falls back to `Hdbscan` with a warning — linfa-clustering doesn't ship GMM in the form we need yet (and petal doesn't have GMM at all). Real GMM support lands when a GMM crate goes through the dep review |
| `cluster-hybrid-outlier-recovery` | partial | `core/src/cluster.rs::build_cluster_tree` — when `algorithm = Hybrid`, every outlier is re-scored against the interim cluster centroids and joins its nearest cluster if cosine ≥ 0.6 (matches the spec's "GMM places with probability > 0.6" gate). When `include_outliers = false`, the threshold is dropped so every outlier is force-routed. Leiden is intentionally **not** wired into Hybrid — Leiden places every point in a community by construction, so the outlier-recovery reassignment has nothing to do; small-community-flagged outliers under `Leiden` still get force-routed when `include_outliers = false`. **Gap:** approximation — the spec calls for a real GMM-soft-membership pass; we use cosine-to-centroid because GMM isn't wired yet (see `cluster-algorithm-selectable`). Soft-member tagging (distinguishing primary vs soft members in the cluster row) is not yet expressed in `BuiltClusterNode` |

Query is L2-normalized on entry; centroids expected pre-normalized at tree-construction time. Top-1/top-2 margin computed across the final beam. The triage producer (`core::suggest::triage_match`) wraps a fresh `LoadedTreeView` over the tree `.md`'s `EditableNode`s and is the first real consumer (Sprint D). `min_confidence` / `min_margin` per-tree thresholds + dynamic beam-width storage on the tree row remain a future tunable; callers pass `beam_width = 2` (spec default) explicitly |

Brute-force O(n²) is consistent with the HDBSCAN path's posture; fine at personal-vault sizes <10k notes |

UI surfaces the original five in the cluster review tab's Advanced disclosure when `algorithm == leiden` (`app/src/panels/cluster_review/mod.rs`); `top_level_resolution` is consumed exclusively by the build recipe's first Split call against the virtual root (`cluster-build-recipe`) and by `Trees::split_cluster`'s virtual-root invocation (`cluster-op-split`) — recursive sub-splits and real-node user-driven splits revert to `resolution`. γ is the Reichardt-Bornholdt configuration parameter; γ > 1 finer, γ < 1 coarser |

Folder-rename / centroid drift on `from-folders` trees may produce surprising overlaps; the threshold is conservative |

Resolves the target (virtual root vs real cluster node) into a leaf set, fetches embeddings via a caller-supplied `Fn(&str) -> Option<Vec<f32>>` resolver (keeps `core::trees` free of `core::store` imports), runs `core::cluster::partition` / `partition_leiden` against the embeddings, inserts one new sub-cluster row per HDBSCAN/Leiden community, and reparents the affected leaves. Recursive sub-split when `params.recurse = true`: each newly-produced child whose member count > `params.leaf_min_size` (default 5) AND whose intra-cluster cohesion radius > `params.leaf_cohesion_threshold` (default 0.15) is recursively split. Hard 16-level cap. Top-level virtual-root Leiden call uses `params.leiden.top_level_resolution` (default 0.3); recursive sub-splits revert to `params.leiden.resolution` (1.0). One `record_split` history row per level. Command `cluster_op_split` lifted to a thin host wrapper. New `ClusterParams` fields: `recurse: bool` (default false, `#[serde(default)]`), `leaf_min_size: u32` (default 5), `leaf_cohesion_threshold: f32` (default 0.15). `min_clusters_to_recurse` retained as `#[serde(default, skip_serializing)]` for back-compat with persisted method JSON. New `LeidenParams.top_level_resolution: f32` (default 0.3, `#[serde(default)]`). `SplitOutcome { new_clusters, total_levels, outliers }` returned. Helpers `core::cluster::mean_normalize` and `core::cluster::ninetieth_percentile_distance` lifted to `pub` for the radius check. **Note:** virtual-root recursion is deferred until the build recipe lands (the children of a virtual-root split don't yet have leaf rows to walk through); top-level virtual-root invocation is supported, and user-driven real-node recursion works end-to-end |

Subtree filter via `SummarizeParams.subtree_root` + `recursive`. Skips leaves; skips `user_edited_name OR user_edited_summary` unless `overwrite_user_edited`. Returns `SummarizePlan { tree_id, scope_kind, enqueued, skipped_user_edited, skipped_fresh }` with `enqueued` in deepest-first submission order. Commands `cluster_summarize(tree_id, params_json)` (generic) and `cluster_regenerate_names(tree_id)` (toolbar `All` scope) — both wrap `plan_summarize_sweep` + handle the async queue submission (Trees stays sync per module discipline). Umbrella `TaskKind::ClusterSummarize { tree_id, scope_kind, n_targets }` at `Priority::High` submitted first; per-cluster `RaptorSummarize` tasks at `Priority::Normal` follow in deepest-first order. Returns `SummarizeSweepOutcome { enqueued, skipped_user_edited, skipped_fresh, queue_row_id }` so the UI can render the queue badge. **Note:** the umbrella task uses the coarse fallback called out in the spec — it's submitted into the queue at High priority but has no per-task completion plumbing wired (the supervision "resolve when N children done" path isn't built yet); the orphan-fail sweep clears it after the synthetic expiry. Acceptable per the spec's "coarser but correct" escape clause |
| `cluster-build-async-pass` | planned | `core::cluster` exposes a streaming build entry that runs the structural pass on a tokio task and emits the event stream defined by `cluster-build-progress-stream`. Cooperative cancellation via a shared atomic checked at every level boundary and on a periodic per-node interval inside the partition loop. The blocking `build_tree_structural` stays as a convenience wrapper for tests and non-UI callers |
| `cluster-build-progress-stream` | planned | Event stream emitted by the async structural pass: `Phase { phase }` (`LoadingEmbeddings` / `PartitioningLevel(u32)` / `Finalizing`), `Counters { items_processed, clusters_found, outliers }`, `ClusterDiscovered { node, parent }`, `Done { tree }`, `Cancelled`, `Failed { error }`. Consumed by the cluster review tab; not part of the public crate API for non-UI callers |
| `cluster-op-rollup` | done | `core/src/trees/ops/rollup.rs::Trees::validate_rollup_inputs` + `Trees::apply_rollup`. Validation step checks every `input_node_id` exists in `tree_id` and has a non-empty `summary` (`MissingSummary` shape returned as a `TreesError` carrying the offending id). The host command `cluster_op_rollup(tree_id, params_json)` embeds each input's summary live via the indexer's `Arc<dyn Embedder>` (`session.indexer.embedder()`; matches the hot-reload posture from `embedder-hot-reload-on-model-change`) using `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` so the fastembed call doesn't park a tokio worker. The persistence step runs `partition` (HDBSCAN) or `partition_leiden` against the summary embeddings, refuses with `RollupOutcome::Refused { reason }` when (a) all inputs land in one community or (b) no inputs merged (every group is a singleton). Otherwise inserts one new `cluster_nodes` row per non-singleton community with `parent_id` = the inputs' common former parent (or `NULL` when split), centroid = L2-normalized mean of summary embeddings, confidence = `0.8` flat placeholder (the partitioner doesn't expose per-community confidence cleanly; called out in the spec). New parent's members get reparented via `reparent_many`. History op `rollup` snapshots `prior_parent_ids` per input + `new_parent_ids` + `new_parent_row_snapshots` for undo. Returns `RollupOutcome` so the UI can surface the refusal reason verbatim. Tagged `// status: cluster-op-rollup` in both files |


## Cluster editor (cluster-editor.md)

Interactive surface for viewing, manually editing, and configuring automation on a cluster tree. Replaces the markdown-proposal flow as the primary review surface for one-shot suggestions; the markdown form remains as the on-disk serialization + audit-log shape. Lands alongside `clustering.md` and `suggestions.md` (post-v1).

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `cluster-editor-sidebar-mode` | done | `app/src/sidebar/clusters/mod.rs` (+ `tree.rs`) renders the open-trees list with inline hierarchical rows (chevron + name + summary preview + member count + policy chip + row menu), expand/collapse per cluster, click a leaf to open the note in the editor pane. New-tree modal + mode menu both anchor off the unified sidebar top row (`+` / `⋯`). Refreshes on vault open alongside the file tree |
| `cluster-editor-pane-expand` | done | `app/src/sidebar/clusters/mod.rs` `[⤢]` button opens / activates a cluster-batch-review tab routed through `app/src/panels/cluster_review/mod.rs`. The pane starts in `cluster-tree` sub-mode and flips to `cluster-batch-review` after Apply |
| `cluster-editor-pane-mode` | done | cluster-batch-review tab kind handled in `app/src/panels/cluster_review/mod.rs`; the workbench host hides the editor + reveals the cluster-editor pane when the active tab is this kind. Toolbar carries Apply / Save-as-triage / Regenerate / Rebuild / view-toggle / Discard. The header bar is styled as proper top chrome and stays pinned to the pane's top edge as the tree body scrolls past. Tree body has full parity with the sidebar's expand/collapse + click-to-edit (name + summary) + right-click context menu (Move to… / Split / Subcluster… / Merge children up / Drop cluster / Send to outliers / Promote out of outliers…) + policy chip popover + ↻ staleness badge + Shift/Cmd/Ctrl-click selection. Multi-select toolbar (Merge siblings / Drop / Stage move to / Stage tag with / Clear) lands in the pane header when there's a selection. Pane-local expanded + selection sets are keyed by tree id so the queue-event-driven refresh preserves user UI state; first-open seeds expanded with root-level cluster nodes |

Result lives on the tab's in-memory state; no tree `.md` is written |

Public surface: `pub fn build_tree_structural`, `pub struct NoopSummarizer`. No LLM client / `[llm].enabled` required for the structural pass |

The freshly persisted node list (post `Trees::insert_nodes`) is filtered to `NodeKind::Cluster && !user_edited_name`, preserving inline-renamed clusters, and inherits the child-then-parent insertion order from `result_to_node_inserts_pub`. The standalone `ReviewMethod` enum and `cfg.method` field were removed. Algorithm choice rides through autosave via `ReviewConfig.algorithm`. Reactive refresh: Leiden surfaces `k_nearest` + `edge_weight_floor` + `resolution` + `iterations` + `min_cluster_size`; HDBSCAN/Hybrid/GMM surface `min_cluster_size` + `min_samples`; FromFolders surfaces `outlier_threshold`. The pane stows the `tokio::sync::mpsc::Receiver<BuildEvent>` in an `Arc<Mutex<>>`; `drain_events` pulls events via `try_recv` at the top of each `show()` call and `ui.ctx().request_repaint()` keeps the frame loop ticking while the build is alive. The blocking `build_tree_structural` shim stays for tests |

Discard / tab-close also flip the atomic so the background task wakes promptly |

Synthesised rows use the spec's encoding rules — placeholder ids, `user_edited_name` reflects the pane's `user_renamed` map, no policy (color), `summary_membership_churn = 0` (no staleness tint). When `effective_requires_review == false` the op is applied immediately (`op_writes::flip_batch_status(accept)`), matching the spec's `status = accepted` direct-apply path; otherwise it stays pending for review. `Freeze` policies and out-of-scope sources skip without emitting an op |

Splits with fewer than two HDBSCAN clusters return an error rather than producing a degenerate single-child fanout. **Gap:** UI invocation is non-recursive (one level only); the recursive Split that drives the build recipe (`cluster-build-recipe`) lands as part of `cluster-op-split` |

Records a distinct `promote-outlier` history op so the log reads naturally. | `cluster-editor-multi-select` | partial | `app/src/sidebar/clusters/mod.rs` (+ `tree.rs`) — Shift / Cmd / Ctrl-click distinctions (split out from the old single "any modifier toggles" path by `cluster-editor-multi-select-shift-range`). Selection survives expand/collapse. When 1+ nodes are selected, the tree header surfaces Merge siblings / Drop / Stage move to… / Stage tag with… / Clear. Stage move/tag (`-stage-move`, `-stage-tag`) land via Sprint C's `core::suggest::stage_*` path. **Partial:** "selecting a cluster includes its subtree implicitly for bulk-action purposes" isn't yet wired — the toolbar reads exactly the selected ids, no descendant expansion. Bulk policy-apply across the selection set is still a follow-up |
| `cluster-editor-multi-select-shift-range` | done | `app/src/sidebar/clusters/tree.rs` — split the legacy "Shift/Cmd/Ctrl all toggle" behavior into three distinct gestures (plain / Cmd-Ctrl / Shift). The anchor is set on every non-shift click; shift-click computes the slice from the flat top-to-bottom display order the renderer rebuilds each paint. Collapsed clusters' descendants are not in the order, so they don't appear in ranges — matches the spec. Ranges cross cluster boundaries because the display-order walk is sibling-and-descendant-flat (the outlier bucket virtual row is included when it has a real node id) |
| `cluster-editor-dnd-reparent` | done | `app/src/sidebar/clusters/tree.rs` — drag-and-drop wired onto every rendered row (both sidebar and expanded pane). Multi-drag: when the dragstart row is part of the current selection, the payload is the whole selection; otherwise just that row. Validation refuses cycles (target in dragged's descendant set), self-drop, leaf-onto-leaf, cluster-onto-current-parent. Cross-tree drag refused at the drop site by comparing tree ids. The drop loops the move per item so each move appends its own `move` history row through `core::trees::move_node`. Promote-to-top zone drops with `newParent = null`, which `core::trees::move_node` natively accepts |
| `cluster-editor-dnd-visual-feedback` | done | `app/src/sidebar/clusters/tree.rs` — render states: a 2px inset accent box-shadow + faint accent tint on valid drop hover, a not-allowed cursor on invalid drop hover, and a floating chip that follows the pointer during drag (rounded white background, 3px corner radius, 1px border per `cluster-editor-graph-view-label-style`; shows "N items" + first glyph for multi-drag, name + glyph for single). Both surface a toast keyed off `SummarizeSweepOutcome.enqueued` / `skipped_fresh` / `skipped_user_edited`: "Enqueued N cluster summaries — watch the queue for progress" when work was queued, "All N already fresh — nothing to do" when the backend's `StaleOrUnfilled` predicate skipped everything (`skipped_fresh` count is reported). Queue rows fan out per `cluster-op-summarize-sweep` |

Disabled when count == 0. Click invokes `cluster_summarize` with `scope = stale-or-unfilled`, `subtree_root = null`, `recursive = true`, `summarize_mode = llm`, `overwrite_user_edited = false`; toast on outcome. | `trees-md-frontmatter` | planned | Frontmatter shape: `hiker.{kind,id,name,source,state,scope,method,created_at,vault_snapshot,nodes}`. `nodes` is a flat list keyed by `id`, each carrying `parent` (omitted on root), `kind`, `note: {id,path}` double-link on leaves, `name`/`summary`, `user_edited_*` (default false), `policy` (omitted when none), `confidence`, `churn`. Unknown keys preserved on round-trip. Centroids are NOT stored here (see `trees-centroids-index`) |
| `trees-centroids-index` | planned | Derived `cluster_centroids` table in `index.db` keyed by `(tree_id, node_id)`, packed little-endian f32; written by the build pass + membership-changing edits, recomputed from member embeddings when missing. Consumed by `cluster-place-beam-descent`. Follows the index's derived-table / fail-loud-on-schema-bump discipline (per `store-version-fail-loud`) |
| `trees-edit-setfrontmatter` | planned | Every tree edit is a frontmatter mutation committed through the op-log working layer as a `SetFrontmatter` op (semantic op name carried on the op `metadata`); no tree-specific `OpKind`. `SetFrontmatter` is the shared substrate for structured-frontmatter writes (trees today, plugin structures later) |
| `trees-module-discipline` | planned | All frontmatter (de)serialization stays behind the `core::trees` boundary; outside callers consume `TreeRow` / `EditableNode` / `NodeKind` / `NodePolicy` — plain serde Rust in `core/src/trees/types.rs`. Mirrors `core::trails` (markdown + frontmatter, no on-disk YAML leakage). No SQLite, no schema-version file, no migration code. Submodule layout: `mod.rs`, `types.rs` (DTOs + `Trees` handle), `store.rs` (frontmatter load/serialize + op-log writes), `ops/{edit,move_node,merge,drop,folder_rename,split,rollup}.rs` |

| `cluster-editor-undo-redo` | planned | in-memory session undo/redo stack; each step applies the reverse frontmatter edit through the working layer (`SetFrontmatter`). Cross-session "revert to an earlier state" rides the tree doc's version history / snapshots, not a bespoke per-tree log |
| `cluster-editor-discard-draft` | planned | `source = one-shot` → delete the tree's `.md` via `core::ops::delete` (lands in trash, restorable like any note); saved-triage → "Unsave as triage" flips `state` back to `draft` and optionally deletes (user chooses) |

| `cluster-editor-markdown-view-editable-parse-back` | removed | superseded by the frontmatter-as-truth model: the body render is read-only and there is no parse-back. Hand-editing the tree `.md`'s frontmatter (an ordinary op-log buffer edit on a regular note) is the text-editing path instead |
| `cluster-editor-apply-action` | done | `core/src/suggest.rs::apply_tree` walks the tree, runs `resolve_effective_policy` per leaf, and emits one `staging.db` row per `Tag` / `Move` leaf — `surface = "cluster-editor"`, `metadata.tree_id`, `metadata.matched_node_id`, `metadata.policy_kind`, `metadata.require_review`, `metadata.tree_member_fingerprint`. Move rows carry `source_path`; Tag rows carry the pre-merged content + `source_hash` for drift safety. Skipped leaves (frozen / unpolicied / missing-note) are counted onto `ApplyOutcome`. `cluster_apply` command + UI `cluster-editor-pane-mode` Apply button + auto-flip of tree `state` → `applied` once every emitted row resolves |
| `cluster-editor-save-as-triage` | done | `app/src/panels/cluster_review/mod.rs` enables the Save-as-triage button when the tree's `state != "saved-as-triage"`. Clicking confirms then calls `cluster_tree_set_state(tree_id, "saved-as-triage")` (Sprint A's `Trees::set_tree_state` free-string setter). Subsequent on-save events fire the classifier against the saved tree. Replaces any prior saved tree implicitly — the spec's one-saved-tree-per-vault rule is enforced by the `state == "saved-as-triage"` predicate (the user re-uses save-as-triage on a different tree to replace) |
| `cluster-editor-regenerate-via-task-queue` | done | `cluster_regenerate_names` host command walks every cluster node, skips fully user-edited ones, and submits one `TaskKind::RaptorSummarize { tree_id, cluster_node_id, level: 0 }` task at `Priority::Normal`. Toolbar "Regenerate names" button + the per-row `↻ N` staleness badge both invoke it. Worker that consumes the task (resolves members → LLM call → writes back through `Trees::set_summary`) rides the existing direct-LLM drain. **Ordering:** tasks are submitted bottom-up — nodes grouped by depth from root (`BTreeMap<usize, Vec<EditableNode>>`), each depth's batch is `await_outcome`'d before the next shallower batch is enqueued, so parent summaries see their children's freshly-generated names instead of placeholders ("Cluster 1", "Cluster 2") |
| `cluster-editor-llm-actions-via-task-queue` | partial | The producer side now routes through `core::tasks`: regenerate (`cluster_regenerate_names`), staleness-badge regenerate, triage classifier dispatch (`cluster_triage_enqueue`). **Partial:** the auto-name-on-merge / auto-name-on-split fan-out is still inline-deterministic naming; promoting those to task submissions is a follow-up tied to the LLM-backed `Summarizer` impl |
| `cluster-editor-triage-on-save` | done | The watcher-subscriber spawn in the host (after `spawn_staging_recheck`) consumes every `FileEvent::Modified` / `Created`, applies the `[suggestions.triage].scope` prefix pre-filter, resolves the note's id + embedding from `read_store`, and runs `core::suggest::triage_all_saved_trees` synchronously (microseconds; no LLM). The async/queued variant is `cluster_triage_enqueue` per `cluster-editor-triage-via-task-queue` |
| `cluster-editor-triage-scheduled-rerun` | partial | the host — `parse_rerun_interval` parses a duration-string grammar (`30m`, `1h`, `6h`, `24h`, `7d`) and a tokio task ticks at the parsed interval, enqueueing one `RaptorTriageMatch` at `Priority::Low` per (saved-as-triage tree × note in scope), driven by the existing `[suggestions.triage].scope` prefix filter. Settings UI row already exists from Sprint D. **Partial:** the spec's grammar is cron-shape (e.g. the spec example `"0 3 * * *"`); the runtime accepts only the duration grammar above and silently logs + disables anything else — a user copy-pasting the documented cron example gets no scheduled rerun. Cron-shape support is tracked as `cluster-editor-triage-scheduled-rerun-cron-syntax` |
| `cluster-editor-triage-scheduled-rerun-cron-syntax` | planned | accept the cron-shape grammar from the spec (`docs/cluster-editor.md` / `docs/suggestions.md` — `scheduled_rerun = "0 3 * * *"`) in `parse_rerun_interval` so the spec's documented example actually schedules. Likely pulls in a cron crate (or hand-rolled 5-field parser) and adapts the tokio ticker to fire on next-occurrence rather than fixed interval |
| `cluster-editor-triage-modified-rerun` | partial | `[suggestions.triage].modified_rerun` (bool, default `false`) + `.modified_rerun_cosine_guard` (float, default `0.15`) added in `core/src/config.rs`; eligible-key registered for both Vault and User scope; surfaced in the settings UI alongside the other triage controls (`app/src/panels/settings/mod.rs`). **Partial:** the runtime guard isn't yet wired — honoring the cosine threshold requires tracking each note's last-triaged embedding (a new `notes.last_triaged_embedding` column or equivalent), which is a follow-up. Today the on-save trigger fires unconditionally within `scope` per `cluster-editor-triage-on-save`; the modified-rerun config is forward-compat plumbing |

The synchronous classifier (`cluster-editor-triage-on-save`) is the on-save fast path; producers wanting queue visibility (CLI parity, scheduled rerun, manual "Re-triage this note") use the async path |

Radial is the default; layout id persisted in per-tree saved view state. The force-directed variant runs `LayoutParams { scaling_ratio: 100.0, outbound_attraction_distribution: false, degree_repulsion: true, .. }` — `outbound_attraction_distribution = false` is the ForceAtlas2 default and is what produces the "leaves sit in a ring around their hub at consistent radius" look; turning it on divides each edge's spring by the source mass, which lets hub leaves drift outward instead of settling at a fixed radius. `scaling_ratio = 100` keeps the settled graph spanning enough world units to render legibly at the default zoom. `tools/graph-snapshot/` is the headless harness for iterating on these tunings against synthetic graphs without launching the app |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-layout-extensible` | done | layout registry in `app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs` keyed by layout id. Adding a layout = new function + registry entry; the renderer stays layout-agnostic (consumes only `x` / `y` on the node) |

| `cluster-editor-graph-view-hover-preview-card` | done | `app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs::paint_nodes` records a `HoveredNode { name, summary, leaf_path, screen_pos }` whenever the cursor is over any cluster or leaf node; the panel renders the in-canvas preview card via the shared `crate::panels::graph::paint_preview_card` helper when `state.show_preview` is on, anchored to `screen_pos` with quadrant-flip + canvas-clamp placement. Body source: leaves resolve `leaf_path` via `store.path_for_id(note_ref)` (with a fallback to using `note_ref` as a path string for the cluster-review embed where note_ref carries the vault-relative path directly) and the cached `selected_preview` is refreshed via `update_selection` on hover; the file body is passed through `panels::graph::skip_frontmatter` so the YAML block is dropped before truncation. Cluster nodes use their in-memory `summary`, falling back to whatever cached leaf body is still around when the summary is empty. Card style: light `#fafafa` background, `#c8cdd4` 1px border, dark text; title laid out with `painter.layout` against `inner.width()` so long basenames wrap inside the card. Vault graph (`app/src/panels/graph.rs::show`) shares the same paint helper and the same hover-to-update flow |
| `cluster-editor-graph-view-no-reshape` | done | No drag-reshape on the canvas (only click + hover + pan/zoom). Footer note when selection is non-empty reminds the user that tree-shape edits stay in the row view |

| `cluster-editor-graph-view-not-vault-graph` | done | The cluster-tree graph view (`app/src/panels/cluster_graph.rs`) consumes the shared egui force-graph widget but its data plumbing (cluster trees + policies) is entirely separate from any future vault-wide graph view (notes + wikilinks). Different panels, shared widget primitive only |
| `cluster-editor-tree-diff-view` | planned | deferred — diff between two cluster trees (e.g. fresh build vs. saved triage) so user can see what shifted |
| `cluster-editor-recluster-subtree` | done | UI now routes through the clustering review tab. Row-menu "Recluster subtree…" entry calls `openClusterReviewTab({ kind: "recluster-subtree", treeId, nodeId, nodeName })`; the tab runs the structural pass via `cluster_run_structural` (recluster_target arm) and on Confirm calls `cluster_op_recluster_subtree_from_built` which replaces the subtree the same shape as the legacy `cluster_op_recluster_subtree` (records `recluster-subtree` history row via `Trees::record_recluster_subtree`, namespaced ids `recluster-<node>-<id>`, leaves re-parent through `reparent_many`, selected node row preserved, `carry_policies_down` copies the resolved policy onto direct children) and submits per-cluster `RaptorSummarize` tasks for non-user-renamed nodes. Legacy `cluster_op_recluster_subtree` command is preserved (unused by UI) for tests / CLI |
| `cluster-editor-recluster-subtree-policy-loss` | done | `cluster_op_recluster_subtree` walks up from the selected node to the root to compute the *resolved* policy (first explicit ancestor's policy wins, including the selected node's own). When the prompt's "Carry policies down from selected node" checkbox is checked (default-off per spec), that resolved policy is copied as an explicit policy onto every new direct child of the selected node. The `recluster-subtree` history row snapshots `prior_subtree` with each descendant's full row shape including its `policy` JSON — `undo_recluster_subtree` re-inserts them via `restore_node_row`, so undo restores the full prior subtree including policies exactly. Carried policy also flows onto the args JSON so redo replays the same carried-policy state |
| `cluster-editor-recluster-subtree-placement-decoupled` | done | the op is structural only — it never calls `core::staging` and never invokes `move_note` against the vault, so no filesystem changes happen as a side effect of reclustering. The selected node's `cluster_nodes` subtree changes shape; the surviving leaves' `note_ref` columns are untouched, so the notes' on-disk paths stay where they were. Future triage classifications fired through `cluster-place-beam-descent` use the new centroids; the existing Apply path (per `cluster-editor-apply-action`) is the explicit way to project the new structure onto disk via `move_note` staging rows. No code change beyond what `cluster-editor-recluster-subtree` already enforces — this slug is the *absence* of the obvious mistake, captured by ensuring the op only mutates `cluster_nodes` |

| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `suggestions-one-shot-flow` | planned | `hiker suggest` builds a tree into a `.md` file under `.hiker/trees/`; user reviews + applies via cluster editor or CLI; never auto-applies |
| `suggestions-proposal-md` | planned | markdown rendering of a tree; the body is a read-only render of the tree's frontmatter; edits to a rendered/exported file are not parsed back |
| `suggestions-markdown-export` | planned | `hiker suggest export <tree-id> [--out <path>] [--format md\|json]` writes the rendering to a file; one-way (use `hiker suggest import <path>` to create a new tree from a file) |
| `suggestions-tree-export-cli` | planned | umbrella for tree export / import / show / set-policy / move CLI verbs |
| `suggestions-apply-cmd` | done | `core/src/suggest.rs::apply_tree(trees, tree_id, vault, store, log, history)` is the shared apply mechanic — now op-log-backed: `Move` leaves stage one cross-document reorg batch of pending `Rename` ops (`op-log-reorg-batch`, author `auto:cluster`), `Tag` leaves stage one pending content op each via `op_writes::stage_auto_content`. Nothing reaches disk until the user accepts in the batch-review pane. The UI path satisfies the slug per `docs/suggestions.md` line 28; the CLI binary lands as the sub-slug `suggestions-apply-cmd-cli` |
| `suggestions-apply-cmd-cli` | planned | sub-slug of `suggestions-apply-cmd` — `hiker suggest apply <tree-id>` CLI surface with `--interactive` / `--accept-all` / `--dry-run`. `cli/src/main.rs` is currently a 3-line stub; this slug covers wiring it onto `core::suggest::apply_tree` |
| `suggestions-rejection-history` | partial | `core/src/suggest.rs::RejectionHistory` owns `.hiker/suggestion-history.json` — per-`(fingerprint, note_path, action)` rows with a 90-day TTL (GC on every write). Cluster-editor batch-review pane calls `cluster_record_rejection` alongside `staging_reject` so the log is populated on every user reject; `apply_tree` consults the log and skips already-rejected rows. **Partial:** the fingerprint is Sprint C's coarse form (`hash(parent_cluster_name + note_path + action)`); the spec's member-set Jaccard recovery is a follow-up. TTL configurability is also deferred |
| `suggestions-mode-move` | done | `core/src/suggest.rs` stages a pending `Rename` op per move (`target_path = <folder>/<basename>`), authored `auto:cluster`, as part of a reorg batch. Skips no-op moves (target == source). Accept applies the `Rename` through the op log (`op_writes::flip_batch_status`), repointing `doc-index.db` + `meta.path`; the filesystem move stays the caller's concern downstream of `core::vault::move_note` |
| `suggestions-mode-tag` | done | `core/src/suggest.rs::merge_tag_into_frontmatter` pre-computes the file content with the tag appended (idempotent — skipped if already present) under the configured `tag_field` (default `hiker.suggested_tags` per spec). The proposal carries `action = "apply_tag"`, the new content, `source_hash` for drift safety, and `metadata.tag_slug` / `tag_field`. Accept path is the existing content-write path (no new code) |
| `suggestions-tag-field-configurable` | planned | `[suggestions] tag_field` config; default `hiker.suggested_tags`, can be set to `tags` to use the regular list |
| `triage-classifier-engine` | done | `core/src/suggest.rs::triage_match` — loads `cluster_nodes` rows, builds a `LoadedTreeView` (the on-disk version of `TreeView`), runs `cluster::place_beam_descent`, walks the matched leaf up via `resolve_effective_policy`, and emits one `staging.db` row per Move/Tag match. `triage_all_saved_trees` iterates every tree where `state == "saved-as-triage"`. Tests: `suggest::tests::triage_emits_move_row_with_triage_surface`, `triage_drops_match_outside_scope`, `triage_agent_author_forces_pending` |
| `triage-staging-proposals` | done | now op-log-backed: `core::suggest::triage_match` stages a pending `Rename` (Move policy) or pending content op (Tag policy) authored `auto:triage` via `op_writes`, applied immediately when no review is required. `Freeze` policies and no-op (target == source) moves skip without emitting an op. Out-of-scope sources (per `[suggestions.triage].scope`) also skip pre-emission |
| `triage-review-required` | done | `core/src/suggest.rs::triage_match` computes `effective_requires_review = policy.require_review || opts.review_required || author_class == Agent` and stamps it on `metadata.require_review`. The accept-side reads the flag to decide whether to auto-accept on insert vs hold pending. `[suggestions.triage].review_required` config key is registered in `ELIGIBLE_USER` + `ELIGIBLE_VAULT` (`core/src/config.rs`) and surfaced in the Settings UI's "Triage" card. The `author = "auto:triage"` stamp on the auto-accepted `core::changes` row is `AUTHOR_AUTO_TRIAGE` (per `triage-author-class`) — consumed by the accept-time auto-accept path when the worker lands |
| `triage-author-class` | partial | Engine supports User vs Agent: `core/src/suggest.rs::NoteAuthorClass { User, Agent }` + `TriageInput.author_class` flow into `effective_requires_review = policy.require_review || opts.review_required || author_class == Agent`, so an Agent classification reliably forces pending. **Gap:** no caller in Sprint D actually emits `Agent` against a real agent write. The on-save watcher spawn (the host ~line 1242) hardcodes `User`; `cluster_triage_run` accepts a string override but the only frontend caller passes nothing; `cluster_triage_enqueue` doesn't even carry the hint on its `RaptorTriageMatch` payload (consumer in `DirectWorkerHandlers::try_handle` also passes `User`). The agent-author detection mechanism (watcher event source vs staging-history recent-agent-write lookup vs an explicit producer-side hint on the task variant) lands with the auto-accept worker / cancellable queue. The `AUTHOR_AUTO_TRIAGE` constant ("auto:triage") is reserved on the auto-accept path; the user-accept path keeps `author = "user"` per `op-log.md` |
| `triage-auto-undo-toast` | planned | toast + 10s Undo fires from the auto-accept path; Undo logs to rejection history |
| `suggestions-folder-pin` | planned | deferred; folder-level pin to exclude from suggestions and triage moves |

Idempotency: shared synchronous membership cache (paths-of-active-trail's-waypoints set) populated via `trail_get` walking `ResolvedWaypoint.children` recursively (cheaper than per-note lookups; one `trail_get` per refresh covers every path in the trail at once); refreshed on active-trail change and any watcher file events under `.hiker/trails/` or against the active trail-doc. Post-append refresh wired via explicit callback (host refreshes the trails view + active-trail waypoint paths) — the watcher-event refresh path can't fire here because `core::trails::append_waypoint` suppresses the watcher for the trail-doc + waypoint-note paths. See resolved bug `bug-add-to-trail-verbs-dont-refresh-panel` (2026-05-10). The `trails_containing_note` command + `trails_containing_note_with_paths` core helper land alongside (used by future polish slugs and the planned `mcp-tool-trails-containing-note`) |
| `trail-draft-from-agent` | planned | — | MCP `trail_create(draft=true)` (and `trail_append_waypoint` against a draft) produces a draft trail; default depends on `agent-write-review-mode`. Replaces the prior "agent writes are bookkeeping only" framing — drafts route through the review surface, not directly into the user's trail dropdown |
| `trail-draft-from-clustering` | planned | — | clustering pipeline can emit draft-trail proposals alongside its existing reorganization proposal when it detects an implicit reading-order chain; opt-in via `[clustering] propose_trails = false` |
| `trail-draft-review-surface` | planned | — | draft trail-docs at `.hiker/trails/drafts/<id>.md` with `hiker.draft: true`; surface in trails panel (banner row with [Accept] [Reject]) and on the activity detail page's Pending filter; Accept moves to `new_trail_dir` + strips flag, Reject hard-deletes trail-doc + waypoint dir. "Show drafts" toggle in the Trails sidebar dropdown reveals drafts in the trails panel |

## Observability (observability.md)

v1 slice is just "init `tracing` and write to a file." Spans, in-app viewer, and the frontend bridge are deferred until file logs stop answering questions.

### v1

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `obs-tracing-baseline` | done | `core/src/observability.rs` (`init_tracing`); the host (`pick_vault` calls it). All prior `eprintln!` in `core::indexer` / `core::store` / `core::watcher` converted to `tracing::{debug,info,warn,error}!` |
| `obs-log-files` | done | `core/src/observability.rs` (`init_tracing`) — file layer writes `<vault>/.hiker/logs/hiker.log` alongside the stderr layer |
| `obs-log-rotation` | done | `core/src/observability.rs` — `tracing_appender::rolling::Builder` with `Rotation::DAILY` + `max_log_files(7)` |
| `obs-error-context` | done | pattern applied throughout core — e.g. `core/src/indexer.rs` upsert err branch uses `error!(error = %e, path = %rel_path, ...)`; no string-interpolated context |
| `obs-no-content` | done | discipline-only; v1 events log paths/reasons/counts only, never note body text. Documented at the top of `core/src/observability.rs` |
| `obs-no-secrets` | done | discipline-only; nothing in v1 logs auth tokens or API keys (no such config exists yet). Documented at the top of `core/src/observability.rs` |

### Deferred (post-v1)

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `obs-spans-pipeline` | planned | spans wrap pipeline stages, not individual fn calls; deferred — adopt when flat events get hard to correlate |
| `obs-env-filter` | planned | `HIKER_LOG` env var drives `EnvFilter`; defaults `info,hiker=debug`; deferred — v1 hardcodes the default |
| `obs-instrument-watcher` | planned | one span per debounced event; raw events at `trace!` only; deferred behind `obs-spans-pipeline` |
| `obs-instrument-indexer` | planned | top-level span per job; child spans for chunk / embed / store; deferred behind `obs-spans-pipeline` |
| `obs-instrument-embed` | planned | span with `batch_size`, elapsed; per-batch event; deferred behind `obs-spans-pipeline` |
| `obs-instrument-store` | planned | slow-query log at >100ms; no per-call span; deferred (slow-query log itself is a fine v1 add if needed) |
| `obs-instrument-cluster` | planned | top-level span on reconcile; per-level child spans; deferred until clustering lands |
| `obs-command-spans` | planned | `#[instrument]` on every host command; deferred behind `obs-spans-pipeline` |
| `obs-frontend-bridge` | done | the egui app and backend share one process, so the frontend logs directly via `tracing::{error,warn,info,debug,trace}!` with a `target` and flattened fields — no IPC bridge. Error / silent-catch sites across the `app` crate route through `tracing` per `bug-ui-errors-not-routed-to-tracing` |
| `obs-log-channel` | planned | custom `tracing` layer fans events to a `tokio::broadcast`; the host emits log events; deferred behind in-app viewer |
| `obs-log-ring-buffer` | planned | server-side ring (default 2000 events) for panel history; `get_log_buffer` command; deferred behind in-app viewer |
| `obs-log-viewer-panel` | planned | collapsible UI panel: live event stream, level + free-text filter, pause/resume, open-log-file button; deferred — file logs cover v1 |
| `obs-test-subscriber` | planned | `core::test_support::init_tracing()` per-test, no global init; deferred until a failing test wants logs |
| `obs-perf-flamegraph` | planned | deferred; one-line `tracing-flame` add when needed |


## Search (search.md)

v2 milestone. Vault-wide hybrid search (lexical FTS5 + semantic) hosted in the repurposed discovery panel; type-ahead with debounce + epoch-cancel; engine traits so tantivy can swap in later. Phases 1–4 landed: backend foundation, panel restructure + persistence, type-ahead + result rendering + click-to-chunk, Ctrl-Space focus + arrow/Enter/Tab/Esc nav. The deferred slugs at the bottom of the table are next; they're scoped follow-ups, not v2 blockers.

| `search-bar-input` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` (search input) |
| `search-mode-toggles` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` (semantic/lexical toggles) — S/L pills next to the input; pressed state same as existing toolbar buttons |
| `search-modes-both-off-disabled` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` (input disabled state) — both toggles off → input disabled + placeholder swaps to "Enable Semantic or Lexical to search" |

| `search-typeahead-debounce` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` — 250ms debounce + monotonically-increasing search epoch; stale responses dropped before render. Empty query short-circuits without scheduling and bumps the epoch so any in-flight call drops |
| `search-empty-collapses-results` | done | `app/src/panels/search.rs` (section visibility) — non-empty query reveals the search section, empty hides it; related section keeps the panel content as before |

Both focus the search input, which expands the panel if collapsed and selects existing input contents. The keybind registry doesn't yet have a `scope` field — the global half lives outside the registry until that refactor lands |

## LLM (llm.md)

All deferred. Lands with the v3.5 ACP-client + bundled-agent milestone in `design.md` build order. Architectural decisions (ACP-only, three feature types, ToS posture, etc.) live in the spec; the slugs below are concrete implementable features.

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `llm-core-module` | done | `core/src/llm.rs` — `LlmClient` trait (`chat` / `chat_stream`) + `GraniteLlmClient` wrapping graniet/`llm`; `ProviderConfig` carries backend/model/api-key-env/base-url/limits; module discipline holds (only place the `llm` crate is imported). `MockLlmClient` for downstream tests. TOML loader for `llm-providers-config` is the next slug |
| `llm-providers-config` | done | `core/src/config.rs` — `[llm]` section in the standard hiker config (per-vault `vault/.hiker/config.toml` with user-scope fallback, deep-merged like every other section): top-level `enabled` (reserved for `llm-disable-mode`), `[llm.provider]` (backend / model / api_key_env / api_key / base_url), `[llm.limits]` (max_tokens / timeout_secs), `[llm.agent]` (iteration_cap / tool_timeout_secs), `[llm.audit] log_full_prompt`. Strict-load validates the section. `core::llm::provider_config_from` + `GraniteLlmClient::from_config` build a usable client from the loaded section. API keys come from one of two sources, in precedence order: literal `[llm.provider].api_key` (user-scope TOML only — eligibility list refuses vault-scope writes so a synced vault can't carry the secret) takes precedence over `api_key_env` when set. The settings pane hides the `api_key` row whenever the section's scope toggle is at `[Vault]`. Empty + empty = no key on the builder (correct for local Ollama). |
| `llm-basic-agent-loop` | done | `core/src/agent.rs` — `run_turn(input, client, dispatcher, cfg, events_tx, cancel)` runs one user-message → terminal-response cycle. `LlmClient` extended with `chat_with_tools` (returns `ToolChatResponse` carrying text + `Vec<ToolCall>`); message history carries `tool_calls` / `tool_results` so multi-turn tool-use is coherent. Tool dispatch goes through the injected `ToolDispatcher` trait — the MCP wiring still lives in `agent-tool-routing-via-mcp` (separate slug). 6 unit tests cover terminal turn, tool-use turn, cap-hit, tool timeout, tool failure, and cancel. |
| `agent-tool-routing-via-mcp` | done | `mcp-server/src/agent_bridge.rs::McpAgentDispatcher` implements `core::agent::ToolDispatcher` against an `Arc<HikerHandler>` exposed via `McpServerHandle::agent_handler()`. `HikerHandler::dispatch_tool(name, args_json)` is the new in-process entry — same `_inner()` helpers the rmcp tools call, same audit log, same error model. `agent_tool_defs()` advertises the seven tools with `schemars`-derived JSON Schemas; the host's chat module injects this dispatcher into `run_turn`. |
| `agent-event-stream-shape` | done | `core/src/agent.rs::AgentEvent` covers the 11 spec variants (`ToolCallArgsDelta` added with the streaming variant). Emitted on `tokio::mpsc::Sender<AgentEvent>` from `core::agent`; the host re-emits each on the chat events channel; `app/src/chat/render.rs` dispatches by `kind` to render text deltas, tool-call cards, the Continue/Stop row, errors, and finish-reason rows. |
| `agent-chat-command-surface` | done | Backend: the host's chat module — `chat_send` / `chat_continue` / `chat_stop` / `chat_cancel` commands; `VaultSession::chat: Arc<ChatRegistry>` holds the spec's `Arc<Mutex<HashMap<TurnId, TurnState>>>` shape (history, tools, system prompt, `StopSignal`, `in_flight` flag, finish flag). Each `chat_send` / `chat_continue` spawns a per-turn tokio task (refused under the registry lock if `in_flight`) that drives `run_turn`, forwards each event through a tiny event-pump task, and writes the new history back so a follow-up resume sees the latest state; an RAII guard wrapping the spawned task clears `in_flight` (and GCs the registry on terminal finish) regardless of how the task exits, so a panic mid-loop can't leak the entry. `chat_continue` re-invokes `run_turn` directly with `user_message: None` per the spec — the cap reset falls out because `run_turn`'s cap is per-call, no empty user message is appended. `chat_stop` fires `StopSignal::user_halt` (→ `FinishReason::UserHalted`); `chat_cancel` fires `StopSignal::cancel` (→ `FinishReason::Cancelled`). Frontend wired up in `app/src/chat/render.rs` (+ `send.rs`) — see `chat-panel-*` rows. |
| `agent-iteration-cap-prompt` | done | core-side: `run_turn` returns `FinishReason::CapHit`, emits `AgentEvent::IterationCapHit`. UI-side: `app/src/chat/render.rs` renders the spec's `"Agent has made N tool calls — [Continue] [Stop]"` row; Continue invokes `chat_continue(turn_id)` (which re-enters `run_turn` with the same accumulated history and a fresh cap budget — exactly "resets to 10 more"); Stop invokes `chat_stop(turn_id)`. |
| `agent-tool-call-timeout` | done | `core/src/agent.rs::dispatch_with_timeout` wraps each `ToolDispatcher::dispatch` call in `tokio::time::timeout` using `cfg.tool_timeout_secs` (default 30 from `[llm.agent] tool_timeout_secs`). On timeout: emits a `ToolResult { ok: false, summary: "tool timed out" }` event and feeds the same shape into history so the model can recover. Never bubbles as a turn-killing error. Cancellation is interleaved via `tokio::select!` so a `chat_cancel` mid-dispatch unblocks the wait without leaking the dispatcher task to its natural completion. |
| `llm-acp-client-optional` | done | `core/src/acp.rs:1`, `core/src/config.rs:137`, the host chat module, `app/src/panels/settings/mod.rs` | `core::acp` module spawns external ACP agent process, creates session with hiker MCP server auto-attached via `McpServer::Http`, translates ACP events to `AgentEvent`. `[acp].agent` config key ("none" / "claude-code" / "goose"), settings pane dropdown. `chat_send`/`chat_continue` branch to `spawn_acp_turn()` when agent != "none". Session resume via ACP's `session/load` not yet supported — agent starts fresh each turn |
| `llm-context-injection` | planned | when hiker has high-confidence relevant context for an interactive turn, inject it as Embedded Resource (ACP) or in-prompt context (basic agent loop) |
| `llm-disable-mode` | planned | `[llm] enabled = false` turns off background + fan-out + chat panel; MCP server stays available |
| `llm-feature-debounce` | planned | 1–2s coalesce window for save-driven LLM features so save bursts → one prompt |
| `llm-prompts-file-store` | done | `core/src/prompts.rs` — two-tier loader: user `~/.config/hiker/prompts/<feature>.md` (auto-created from bundled defaults on first run via `ensure_user_default`), vault `vault/.hiker/prompts/<feature>.md` (vault wins). `Prompts::load(vault_root)` produces an in-memory store; `bundled_defaults()` registers the v3.5 features (currently `chat_system` for the basic agent loop's system prompt). `the host's `prepare_for_turn`` uses it to render the system prompt with `{{vault_name}}` substituted. |
| `llm-prompts-mustache-templating` | done | `core/src/prompts.rs::substitute` — `{{var}}` substitution (whitespace-tolerant). Unknown placeholders pass through verbatim rather than blanking, so a partially-customized prompt doesn't silently lose data. Each shipped default starts with an HTML-comment header naming its placeholders. |
| `llm-prompts-staleness-on-upgrade` | done | `core/src/prompts.rs::Prompts::staleness` — sidecar `<feature>.default.sha` next to each user-scope prompt records the bundled default's blake3 hash at first-write. Bumping the bundled default upstream surfaces as a stale-feature name from `staleness()`; the user file is never clobbered. the host (`open_vault_at_inner`) calls `staleness()` once per session and emits both a `tracing::warn!` and an `AgentLog` row (`surface = "core::agent"`, `status = "stale_prompt"`) per stale feature, so the future Prompts-tab can read either surface. |
| `llm-prompts-settings-tab` | planned | settings UI Prompts tab: editable text, default reference, reset, diff, test affordance |
| `llm-prompt-test-button` | planned | "test prompt with sample data" affordance in Prompts tab |
| `llm-audit-log` | done | `core/src/audit.rs` — `AgentLog` JSONL writer at `<vault>/.hiker/agent-log/<YYYY-MM-DD>.jsonl` with daily rotation, single mutex per writer, content-blind once `record` is called. `AuditEntry` carries `surface` (`core::agent` / `core::llm` / `core::acp` / `mcp-tool-call`), `feature`, `status`, optional `error`, optional `turn_id` / `step_id`, and free-form `details` so debugging trails can correlate panel events with audit rows. Wired three places: `core::agent::run_turn` writes one row per step (status ok / error, tool-call count, finish reason; full text only when `log_full_prompt = true`); `mcp-server/src/audit.rs` is now a thin wrapper over the shared writer carrying the existing `[mcp.audit] log_full_input` redaction policy with `surface = "mcp-tool-call"`; the host records `stale_prompt` rows for `Prompts::staleness` hits. The shared writer means `[mcp.audit] log_full_input` and `[llm.audit] log_full_prompt` land their rows in the same daily file. Background / fan-out / ACP surfaces will plug in by passing `Some(AgentAudit { ... })` (or constructing their own `AuditEntry`) when those slugs land. |

| `chat-panel-pinned-bottom` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` lays out the chat region pinned to the bottom of the discovery panel below the search/related sections, with a resize handle above it; chat expands upward as the user drags the handle. Same column as search/related, the same discovery toggle flips the whole panel; when LLM is disabled, the chat region and handle are removed so sections fill the panel. |
| `chat-panel-detached-scroll` | done | `#discovery-sections` and `.chat-transcript` each have their own `overflow-y: auto` + `min-height: 0`, so the sections region and chat transcript scroll independently. The drag-handle divider visually separates them; scrolling the transcript doesn't move search results out of view, and vice versa. |
| `chat-panel-vertical-resize` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` (resize-handle drag). Hover swaps the cursor to row-resize; drag updates the chat region to a clamped `[0.1, 0.9]` fraction of the discovery panel's height; pointer-up persists the fraction via `set_setting("vault.chat_height", ...)`. The handle highlights while active. |
| `chat-panel-drag-uncollapse` | planned | — | drag-up on the collapsed chat header continues as a resize gesture: pointer-down captures, expands the chat region past a small threshold, and from there drives `chat-panel-vertical-resize` as if the user had grabbed the handle of an already-open region. Single gesture replaces "click to uncollapse, then grab the handle." Pointer-up persists the new height via `chat-panel-default-height` write-back |
| `chat-session-persisted-history` | done | the host's chat module (`ChatRegistry::inner` now `HashMap<SessionId, SessionState>` with an `active` slot), `core/src/sessions.rs` (id + on-disk store). `SessionState.history` accumulates across `chat_send`; the spawned task only writes back via the entry mutex (no per-turn GC). `chat_send` / `chat_continue` / `chat_stop` / `chat_cancel` take `(session_id, turn_id)`; the lazy-create path lives in `resolve_or_create_session`. Fixes `bug-chat-context-lost-across-user-turns` |
| `chat-session-new-button` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` (new-session button next to the chat collapse chevron, invokes `chat_session_new`), `app/src/keybinds.rs` (keybind `chat.new-session` = `Mod-Shift-n`). Backend command is `chat::chat_session_new` which creates a fresh on-disk file via `core::sessions::create_session_file` and pins it as active |
| `chat-session-markdown-store` | partial | `core/src/sessions.rs` — `<vault>/.hiker/sessions/<YYYY-MM-DD>-<id>.md` with YAML frontmatter (id / created_at / model / provider) + alternating `## User` / `## Agent` sections; tool calls render as fenced ```hiker-tool-call``` JSON blocks under the agent section. `chat::persist_turn` appends on every terminal `TurnFinished` and enqueues `IndexJob::Upsert` so search picks the new turn up. Markdown is the source of truth — `parse_session` reconstructs a synthetic alternating-history cache on resume. **Partial as of 2026-05-13**: spec evolved to require `parse_session` to round-trip `hiker-tool-call` + `hiker-tool-result` fenced blocks back into rehydrated history (per `bug-chat-session-resume-drops-tool-calls`); current code drops tool-call structure, leading the agent to think it can write notes without calling tools. Fix lands with the bug |

The "Sessions" virtual-group rendering is one consumer of `tree-source-visibility-toggles` (the registry), which owns that rendering pass for every category — not duplicated here |
| `chat-session-resume-latest` | done | `the host's `resume_latest_at_open`` runs at vault open over `core::sessions::most_recent_session`, hydrates a `SessionState` from `parse_session`, and pins it as the registry's active id. Frontend calls `chat_session_active` post-vault-open and renders the resumed transcript via `chatPanel.hydrate(...)`. New session is the only way to opt out of the latest-session resume; explicit re-open of older sessions rides `chat-session-list-ui` (now done) |
| `chat-session-list-ui` | done | landed as a compact dropdown on the left of the chat resize handle (popover built in `app/src/chat/render.rs`) rather than a sidebar list. Per-row trash icon ships under `chat-session-trash`. Re-open + delete cover the load-bearing surface; the speculative branch affordance is dropped from this slug |

Trash icon on each row in the session-picker dropdown (`app/src/chat/render.rs`); revealed on row hover so the menu reads cleanly when scanning. Restore + permanent delete reuse the regular trash-bin commands — restoring puts the file back at `.hiker/sessions/<date>-<id>.md` and the dropdown surfaces it on next open |

Backend `the host's `spawn_turn_task`` injects the note as a turn-scoped synthetic user-context message at the top of `SessionState.history` *only* on user-driven sends (Continue skips). `last_active_note` collapses consecutive duplicates to a "still viewing" reference. ACP path will plug in via Embedded Resource ContentBlock when `core::acp` lands |

Frontend parses `@selection` / `@<rel-path>`, resolves them at submit time (`chat_resolve_at_note` for notes, the live selection for the highlight), composes the active note + explicit mentions into a single ordered + de-duplicated context-blocks list, and rides the new `chat_send` parameter into `spawn_turn_task` which appends one synthetic user-context message per block. ACP path lands its Embedded Resource ContentBlock shape when `core::acp` does |
| `chat-input-at-selection` | done | `app/src/chat/send.rs` (`selection` token kind + active-selection capture: rel-path + 1-based line range, `null` for empty / preview buffers; selection block never de-duped) | the captured selection rides as a `selection`-kind block carrying source rel-path + `L<a>-L<b>` line range + selected text. Empty / no-editor selection at submit time toasts "@selection has no selected text" and aborts the send so the user can fix the input |
| `chat-input-at-note` | done | `app/src/chat/send.rs` (`note` token kind with rel-path-without-extension), `the host's `chat_resolve_at_note`` (probes `.md` / `.markdown` / `.txt`) | resolves `@<rel-path-without-extension>` against the vault at submit time; on miss the backend error ("note not found: <rel>") flows through the toast and aborts the send |
| `chat-input-at-mentions-dedup` | done | `app/src/chat/send.rs` (seen-rel-paths set; first-seen wins; selection never de-duped) | active-note + every `@<rel-path>` collapse to one block per unique rel-path (including the active-note auto-inject). `@selection` is excluded from the de-dup set since the slice is the point. Implementation drops subsequent dupes outright per the implementation plan; the spec's "see prior block for `<path>`" refinement is equivalent for the model and can be added later if needed |

Backspace at the tail of an inserted token deletes the whole token range as a single edit. Suppressed inside fenced code blocks (single-line and multi-line) and after a `\@` escape |
| `chat-input-at-autocomplete-cmd` | done | `core/src/store.rs::Store::at_autocomplete` + `AtSuggestion` DTO | empty prefix → top-N notes by `last_accessed_at DESC` (NULLs last); non-empty → case-insensitive basename substring with prefix-match-quality + recency ranking; skipped rows excluded; `rel_path` returned with the indexable extension stripped to match the token format. The `selection` synthetic entry is added frontend-side. 2 unit tests in `core/src/store.rs` cover ordering / disambiguation / skipped-row exclusion |

Reverting to the Send affordance happens on `TurnFinished` / `IterationCapHit` / `error`, so cap-hit rows fall back to their existing Continue/Stop buttons. |

Inserted on send / after a tool result; removed on the first text delta / tool-call start of the upcoming step, on cap-hit, on `TurnFinished`, on Error, and on hydrate / reset. The spec asked for a "Thinking…" spinner; we substituted the pulsing-dots shape because it reads better in the compact panel and matches the rest of the UI's visual language |

The bubble view is read-only and only structure the renderer itself emits is shown — no raw HTML pass-through. In-vault link clicks (`hiker://note/<rel>` or bare relative paths) route through the open-note-link path; external `http(s)`/`file` links open externally; other schemes are inert. The editor chrome (background, padding, focus outline, caret) is stripped so the bubble blends in. |
| `chat-panel-note-link-render` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` — link clicks inside the per-bubble view resolve the enclosing link node, extract the URL, and route in-vault targets (bare relative paths or `hiker://note/<rel>`) through the open-note-link path (which respects `file-switch-guard-dirty`). External `http(s)://` / `file://` links open externally. Other schemes are inert. The system-prompt advertise-`hiker://note/<rel>` half lives in `core/prompts/chat_system.md` once that prompt is updated; the click handler accepts both forms today. |
| `chat-panel-tool-call-collapsible` | done | `app/src/chat/render.rs` — tool-call cards render in a one-line `▸ <name>(<short args>) <glyph> — <result>` shape; click expands to a pretty-printed JSON view with a Copy button. The short-args summary walks the parsed JSON to build a deterministic ≤80-char summary (first 1–2 fields, value-truncated); falls back to a whitespace-collapsed slice when the args don't parse cleanly. Per-card expanded state, not persisted. Errored calls (`ok = false`) stay collapsed by default; the ✗ glyph + result summary is enough at a glance. |
| `chat-tool-call-opens-touched-note` | planned | — | tool-call card header (click target separate from the expand-chevron) opens the touched note in the editor for note-touching tool variants (`get_note`, `write_note`, `set_frontmatter`, `apply_tag`, `remove_tag`, future `modify_note`). Resolves the rel-path from the tool result (or args fallback); for multi-note touches, header-click opens a picker. Honors `file-switch-guard-dirty`. Pairs with the trail-from-llm-trace future feature for query auditing |
| `chat-retry-from-user-message` | planned | — | circular-arrow icon-button on each user message; click rolls session back to immediately before that message and starts a fresh turn with the same text. Disabled while a turn is in flight. Available on both `core::agent` and `core::acp` paths |
| `chat-retry-truncates-session-history` | planned | — | retry trims every turn at-or-after the retried user message from `SessionState`; markdown file at `vault/.hiker/sessions/<…>.md` is rewritten to match (markdown is truth per `chat-session-markdown-store`); one `core::changes` row appended for the truncation. No hidden-branch archive in v1 |
| `chat-retry-resubmits-verbatim` | planned | — | retried turn carries the exact original user message text including `@`-mentions; context blocks (active note, `@<note-path>`, `@selection`) re-resolve at submit time. Message is not prefilled into the input — edit-and-resend is a separate future affordance |
| `chat-retry-command` | planned | — | new `chat_retry(session_id, user_message_id) -> Result<TurnId>` on the agent command surface. Implementation: truncate state + markdown → emit `TurnStarted` for the new id → invoke the same turn-driver as `chat_send`. No confirmation modal — the UI rollback animation conveys destructiveness |
| `chat-retry-acp` | planned | — | ACP path maps retry onto ending the current `session/prompt` line and starting a new one with the truncated history; falls back to spawning a fresh ACP session seeded with the pre-retry turns when the configured agent doesn't model history truncation natively |
| `chat-edit-and-resend` | planned | — | deferred — pencil-icon affordance on a user message drops its text back into the input for editing; same destructive trim as retry, but the resend is manual |
| `chat-retry-branches-session` | planned | — | deferred — keep the discarded tail as a named branch of the session so the user can compare outcomes |
| `chat-regenerate-assistant-message` | planned | — | deferred — regenerate-this-response button on an assistant bubble; same `chat_retry` plumbing anchored on the prior user turn |
| `chat-panel-default-height` | done | `core::config::VaultConfig::chat_height` (default `0.30`); `core::config::ELIGIBLE_VAULT` accepts the new `vault.chat_height` key with `ValueType::UnitFraction` validation (rejects values outside `[0.0, 1.0]`); `set_setting` persists via `toml_edit` so user comments survive. `app/src/chat/render.rs` reads `vault.chat_height` after settings load and applies the persisted fraction on vault open. |
| `chat-panel-expand-to-editor` | partial | `app/src/chat/render.rs` (expand button → open agent tab), `app/src/panels/agent.rs` (`agent`-kind tab body), `app/src/workbench_host.rs` (agent branch + agent-key autosave restore) | basic wiring landed: expand button → opens an `agent`-kind tab; the bottom chat region + resize handle hide while it's open. **Partial**: full expand-to-editor experience (session body rendered in tab, chat-region collapse/restore on agent-tab open/close) is still planned; current wiring is the foundations. |
| `chat-session-import-storage-path` | planned | imports land at `vault/.hiker/sessions/imported/<source>-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<id>.md`; same on-disk format as native sessions per `chat-session-markdown-store`; watcher carve-out extends to cover the `imported/` subfolder so imports are indexed and search-reachable |
| `chat-session-import-converters` | planned | `core::sessions::import::<source>` per format; v1 candidates: Claude Code transcript JSON, Codex export, ChatGPT data-export JSON, Goose session log; preserves tool-call structure into hiker's `hiker-tool-call` block when the source carries it, otherwise lossy-text |
| `chat-session-import-sidecar` | planned | entry points: `hiker session import <path>` CLI + settings-UI affordance; sniff → convert → write → index; idempotent on `(source, source_id)` so re-imports update in place |
| `chat-session-imported-show-in-tree-toggle` | planned | `vault.show_imported_sessions_in_tree` (default `false`); plugs into `tree-source-visibility-toggles` registry; separate from `chat-session-show-in-tree-toggle` so users can surface native vs. imported independently |
